## Scope
1. **`mc-admin`** (`src/mission_control/admin_cli.py`, Typer, entry point `mc-admin` in `[project.scripts]` — you may add exactly that one line to `pyproject.toml`): `init-db` (create the schema), `seed [--reset]` (load the PRD §9 data with every demo user's password set to the hashed demo passphrase "orbital launch window"; `--reset` deletes and recreates the database file; without `--reset` on a non-empty database, refuse clearly rather than duplicating data), `create-org` (an operator command creating an organisation and its first Director, per PRD T3 — prompt for the Director's password hidden and twice when interactive, read from stdin otherwise; never as a flag; enforce the 15-character minimum through the existing domain/services rules). Tests first in `tests/cli/test_admin_cli.py` (temp database files only; never touch a real home or the repo root database).

2. **`README.md`** at the repo root, written for an assessor who has just cloned the repo: what it is (two sentences), prerequisites (Python 3.12, uv), quick start (`uv sync`, `uv run mc-admin seed --reset`, `uv run mc-server`, `uv run mc login --email lee@aurora.example` with the demo passphrase), a short guided demo of the S1 golden path through the real `mc` commands (match/apply, accept as each crew member, submit, approvals by Marco and Ingrid with Ruth refused as creator, complete, history) plus one isolation example, the demo accounts table (from PRD §9 / seed data: name, email, role, org), settings (`MC_DATABASE_PATH`, `MC_TOKEN_TTL_HOURS`, `MC_DB_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MC_API_URL`), how to run tests and lint, a map of the docs (brief, PRD, technical design, phase reviews, cli-ux, backlog) and of the code layout (one line per layer), and where the AI transcripts and process are (CLAUDE.md workflow, phase reviews). 3. **README smoke test** (design §9): an automated test that runs the README's quick-start and demo commands for real. On your branch `mc` and `mc-server` don't exist yet, so write it so it (a) extracts or mirrors the README command list in one place, (b) runs `mc-admin` steps fully now, and (c) skips the server/`mc` steps with a clear reason when `mission_control.cli` / the API routers aren't importable — the lead will run it un-skipped after reconciling Phase 4. [project]
name = "mission-control"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Multi-tenant mission planning API and CLI"
requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"
dependencies = [
    "argon2-cffi>=25.1.0",
    "fastapi>=0.115",
    "httpx2>=2.13",
    "rich>=13",
    "typer>=0.12",
    "uvicorn>=0.30",
]

[project.scripts]
mc-server = "mission_control.api.app:main"

[dependency-groups]
dev = [
    "pytest>=8",
    "ruff>=0.6",
]

[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]
packages = ["src/mission_control"]

[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["tests"]
addopts = "-q"

[tool.ruff]
line-length = 100
target-version = "py312"
src = ["src", "tests"]
extend-exclude = ["docs", ".claude"]

[tool.ruff.lint]
select = ["E", "F", "I", "B", "UP"]

# 👩‍🚀 Mission Control — Senior Software Engineer Challenge

> 🧠 **Our philosophy:** We're not testing whether you can memorise syntax or generate the largest application. We're testing how you frame problems, make technical decisions, communicate an implementation direction, direct an agent, and verify what it produces.

## 🎯 The Challenge

Space organisations need to plan complex missions requiring specific crew capabilities. Crew assignment is currently manual and error-prone — leads spend hours cross-referencing skill profiles, availability calendars, and existing commitments. Multiple organisations will use this platform, each with different crew sizes, skill taxonomies, and approval processes.

Mission Control is a B2B platform that helps organisations manage missions and intelligently assign crew based on skills, availability, and workload. Your task is to design and build its core.

Before implementation, produce the design document you would use to communicate the intended solution to an engineering team and guide an AI coding agent. Submit that document with your implementation and transcripts.

You have broad latitude over the product design, data model, technical architecture, and the form and structure of the design document. We're looking for a solution you'd be comfortable putting in front of a customer.

**Minimum scope:** A multi-tenant API with roles, a mission lifecycle and approval workflow, crew management with skill profiles, an auto-matching engine, and a CLI through which the primary workflows can be exercised.

### What We Expect

A working system that demonstrates senior-level judgment. The design document should establish a clear and implementable technical direction without being constrained to a prescribed format. The data model should be well-considered, and the matching algorithm should show real thought about the problem space. The API and CLI workflows should feel coherent and intentional. The code should be structured for a team to work in, not just for a demo to run.

The CLI UX is an important component that would be assessed as part of the usability review.

A web interface is not required.

## 📋 What to Submit

You are required to use AI development tools (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, or equivalent). The design document and transcripts are first-class parts of the submission.

- **Your initial design document** — prepared before implementation and used to guide your AI coding agent. You are welcome to brainstorm this document with AI; in this case, include the process in your transcripts
- **A working API and CLI** — easy to run locally, with clear setup instructions and representative seed data
- **Source code** — a GitHub repository (public or shared with us) with meaningful commit history
- **Full AI transcripts** — every conversation with your AI coding tool during the challenge, unedited

> ⏱️ **Time expectation:** 3–5 hours of focused work. We respect your time—submit what you have. Prioritise a coherent design, a working vertical slice, and evidence of verification over breadth or polish.

> 🛠️ **Tech stack:** Your choice. We use TypeScript, React, Python, and GCP internally — but use whatever you're most effective with. The tools and architectural decisions you make are part of what we're evaluating.

> 🔑 **API keys:** If you need API keys for Claude, we're happy to provide them — just reach out to our friendly team.

> 📩 **Timeline:** There is no hard deadline, but we ask that you submit within 7 days of receiving the challenge.

> ❓ **Questions:** If anything in the brief is unclear, email us. Ambiguity in the brief is sometimes intentional — how you resolve it is part of what we're evaluating.

## 🌍 The Domain

Mission Control is a multi-tenant B2B platform for space organisations. Here's the world you're building for:

- **Organisations** are your tenants — space agencies, research labs, private companies. All data is strictly scoped to an organisation. Data must never leak across tenants.
- **Crew Members** belong to an organisation. They have skill profiles, availability, and assignment history. How you model skills and proficiency is up to you.
- **Missions** belong to an organisation. They have requirements, timelines, and a lifecycle that includes some form of approval before going active. How you design the lifecycle and its transitions is a design decision we're interested in.
- **Assignments** connect crew to missions. The platform should include an auto-matching engine that intelligently suggests crew for missions based on skills, availability, and constraints. The sophistication of this algorithm is part of what we're evaluating.

### Roles

The platform has three user roles with different levels of access:

- **Directors** run the organisation. They manage settings, approve missions, and have broad visibility.
- **Mission Leads** plan and manage missions. They define requirements, run the matcher, and submit missions for approval. They should not be able to approve their own missions.
- **Crew Members** manage their own profiles, availability, and respond to assignments. They have limited visibility into the broader organisation.

## 📝 About the Design Document and AI Transcripts

The design document and transcripts are first-class evaluation artifacts — as important as the code itself. We want to understand how you translated the brief into a technical direction, how you used that direction to guide your AI tool, and how you evaluated what it produced.

We are intentionally not prescribing the structure of the design document. Its scope, organisation, level of detail, and usefulness as implementation context are part of what we're evaluating.

Don't edit the transcripts. We want to see your real process, including dead ends, changes of direction, and mistakes.

## 🔜 What Happens Next

If you pass the async stage, you'll be invited to a 90-minute live session that builds on your submission. Here's what to expect:

- **Product and architecture deep-dive (30 min):** Present your solution as if pitching it to the engineering team. Walk us through the design document, where implementation diverged from it, and the product and technical trade-offs you made. We'll challenge your decisions and ask how you used the design to guide and constrain the agent.
- **Novel system design (40 min):** Fresh design problems, whiteboard-style. We'll ask you to extend the architecture on the fly — for example, how you'd layer in resource constraints or redesign approval workflows.
- **Team and process discussion (20 min):** How you'd break down work, delegate, review AI-generated code, and set guardrails.

Good luck. We're excited to see how you think. 🚀       9 docs/backlog.md

1	# Mission Control — Product Requirements (v1)
2	
3	**Status:** Agreed · **Source of truth:** [`brief.md`](brief.md) · **Next:** technical design (frameworks, schema, API and CLI design)
4	
5	Rules tagged **[B]** come from the brief. Everything untagged is a product decision made while resolving the brief's open questions.
6	
7	## 1. Problem and goals
8	
9	Crew assignment is manual and error-prone. Leads cross-reference skills, availability and existing commitments by hand. **[B]** Mission Control is a multi-tenant B2B platform for managing missions and crew, with an auto-matcher that suggests crew. **[B]**
10	
11	v1 succeeds when:
12	
13	1. A Lead can take a mission from requirements to an active, confirmed roster entirely through the CLI.
14	2. Matcher suggestions never break a hard rule and are deterministic.
15	3. The system enforces the approval rules.
16	4. No read or write can reach another organisation's data.
17	
18	**How we'll know:** every acceptance criterion in §8 passes as an automated test against the seed data in §9.
19	
20	## 2. Requirements from the brief
21	
22	- R1: Organisations are tenants. All data is scoped to one organisation and never leaks across tenants.
23	- R2: Three roles: Director, Mission Lead, Crew Member.
24	- R3: Leads can't approve their own missions.
25	- R4: Missions have requirements, a timeline, and a lifecycle with approval before going active.
26	- R5: Crew have skill profiles, availability and assignment history.
27	- R6: Assignments connect crew to missions, and crew respond to them.
28	- R7: An auto-matcher suggests crew based on skills, availability and workload.
29	- R8: Organisations differ in crew size, skill taxonomy and approval process.
30	- R9: A multi-tenant API, plus a CLI for the main workflows. CLI usability is assessed.
31	- R10: Runs locally with seed data.
32	
33	## 3. Users and roles
34	
35	| Role | Responsibilities |
36	|---|---|
37	| **Director** | Manages users, roles, crew profiles, the skill catalogue and the approval setting. Creates missions for a Lead. Approves or rejects missions. Reassigns owners. Sees everything in their organisation. |
38	| **Mission Lead** | Owns missions. Defines slots, runs the matcher, proposes crew, submits. Maintains other people's skill levels. Reads all missions and crew profiles. |
39	| **Crew Member** | Manages own personal details and unavailability. Accepts, declines or withdraws from assignments. Sees only their own data and the missions they're on. |
40	
41	- Each user belongs to exactly one organisation. The organisation is taken from the user's identity, never from the request.
42	- Each user has exactly one role, which controls permissions.
43	- Taking part in missions depends on a separate **crew profile**. Every Crew Member has one, and Leads and Directors may have one too.
44	
45	## 4. Main workflows (all available through the CLI)
46	
47	1. **Sign in** with email and password, and change your own password. See what needs attention: pending assignments, approvals waiting on me, my missions.
48	2. **Director admin:** manage users and roles, crew profiles, the skill catalogue, and the number of approvals required.
49	3. **Lead or Director:** set crew skill levels (1–5).
50	4. **Crew profile holder:** maintain personal details and unavailability.
51	5. **Create a mission:** name, dates, and role slots with required skills.
52	6. **Owning Lead:** run the matcher, review the suggested roster, apply it or propose crew by hand.
53	7. **Crew:** accept or decline proposals, and withdraw if needed.
54	8. **Owning Lead:** submit a fully accepted roster for approval.
55	9. **Eligible Directors:** approve or reject. The final approval activates the mission.
56	10. **Owning Lead or Director:** complete or cancel a mission.
57	11. **Director:** reassign a mission's owning Lead.
58	12. **View history:** mission history and crew assignment history.
59	
60	## 5. Business rules
61	
62	### 5.1 Definitions
63	
64	| Term | Meaning |
65	|---|---|
66	| **Open assignment** | An assignment that is Proposed or Accepted. |
67	| **Open places** | A slot's headcount minus its open assignments. |
68	| **Mission-days** | The number of days from the start date to the end date, counting both. |
69	| **Overlap** | Two date ranges overlap if they share at least one calendar day. Dates have no time and no time zone. |
70	| **Eligible (crew for a slot)** | The person is active, has a crew profile, and has every required skill at or above its minimum level. They have no unavailability overlapping the mission, and no accepted assignment on another non-final mission that overlaps it. |
71	| **Eligible approver** | An active Director who did not create the mission and is not on its roster. |
72	
73	### 5.2 Tenancy and identity
74	
75	- **T1** Every record belongs to one organisation. Every read and write is limited to the caller's organisation. **[B]**
76	- **T2** Naming a resource from another organisation gives the same response as naming one that doesn't exist ("not found").
77	- **T3** Organisations are created only through seed data or an operator command. There is no self-service signup.
78	
79	### 5.3 People and skills
80	
81	- **P1** Each organisation has its own flat skill catalogue, managed by Directors. Skills with the same name in different organisations are unrelated.
82	  - Directors can rename a skill. Its identity stays the same.
83	  - Directors can delete a skill only if no crew skill or slot requirement uses it.
84	- **P2** Proficiency is an integer from 1 to 5.
85	- **P3** Leads and Directors set and remove skill levels. **Nobody can change their own levels.**
86	  - A skill change is allowed even if it makes someone ineligible for an assignment on a Draft or Pending mission. Eligibility is checked again at acceptance, submission and approval.
87	  - A skill change that would make someone ineligible for their assignment on an Active mission is refused.
88	- **P4** Crew profile holders manage their own personal details and unavailability. Nobody else edits them. Personal details are a display name and an optional bio.
89	- **P5** Crew are available by default. Unavailability is a date range with a reason.
90	- **P6** A crew member can't record unavailability that overlaps one of their accepted assignments.
91	- **P7** Users are deactivated, never deleted. Deactivated users can't sign in, aren't eligible for matching, and keep their history. Deactivation is refused while the user:
92	  - has open assignments; or
93	  - owns non-final missions. The same applies to changing a mission owner's role away from Lead. Their missions must be reassigned first.
94	- **P8** An organisation must always have at least one active Director.
95	- **P9** Crew profiles survive role changes and can't be deleted. Changing a user's role to Crew Member gives them a profile if they don't already have one.
96	- **P10** Directors set a new user's initial password. Users change their own password by entering their current one. There's no email or password-recovery flow.
97	  - Passwords must be at least 15 characters. Passphrases are welcome, and uppercase letters, numbers and symbols aren't required.
98	  - Changing a password ends all of that user's sessions, including the one used to change it.
99	
100	### 5.4 Missions and lifecycle
101	
102	- **M1** A mission has a name, a date range, one owning Lead and a separately recorded creator.
103	  - Leads create missions for themselves. Directors create them on behalf of a Lead.
104	  - Only Mission Leads can own missions.
105	  - Directors can transfer ownership of a non-final mission to another active Lead in the same organisation. If the mission is Pending, it returns to Draft and its approvals are cleared.
106	- **M2** A mission has one or more slots. Each slot has a name, a headcount of at least 1, and at least one required skill with a minimum level. Dates may be in the past, but the end date must be on or after the start date. The same rule applies to unavailability.
107	- **M3** Only the owning Lead can edit a mission, propose or remove crew, apply matcher results, or submit.
108	
109	```
110	         submit                N-th approval                complete
111	 Draft ─────────▶ Pending ───────────────────▶ Active ─────────────▶ Completed
112	   ▲              Approval
113	   └── reject (reason) / any change ──┘
114	 Draft | Pending Approval | Active ──cancel (reason)──▶ Cancelled
115	```
116	
117	| Transition | Who | Conditions and effects |
118	|---|---|---|
119	| Draft → Pending Approval | Owning Lead | All of these must hold: every slot is full, every assignment is Accepted, every rostered crew member is still eligible, and there are at least N eligible approvers. Otherwise the submission is refused with a list of the problems. |
120	| Pending → Active | The N-th distinct eligible approver | The roster is checked again at each approval. If the check fails, the approval is refused and the mission stays Pending, unchanged. |
121	| Pending → Draft | Eligible Director (reject) or system | A rejection needs a reason. Any change to dates, slots, roster or owner, including a crew withdrawal, also sends the mission back to Draft. Approvals are cleared, and remaining accepted assignments stay accepted. |
122	| Active → Completed | Owning Lead or Director | Accepted assignments become Completed. |
123	| Any non-final → Cancelled | Owning Lead or any Director | Reason required. Open assignments become Cancelled. |
124	
125	- **M4** Draft missions can have unfilled places and unanswered proposals.
126	- **M5** Once a mission is Active, its dates, requirements and roster are frozen. Only completion or cancellation are allowed.
127	- **M6** Dates never trigger transitions on their own. There is no scheduler.
128	- **M7** An edit is refused if it would invalidate an open assignment. The response lists the affected crew, and the Lead must remove them first. Crew are never removed automatically. Edits that invalidate an assignment are:
129	  - removing a slot that has open assignments;
130	  - lowering a slot's headcount below its open assignments;
131	  - changing dates or requirements so that an assigned person is no longer eligible.
132	
133	### 5.5 Approval
134	
135	- **A1** Each organisation sets the number of approvals required (N ≥ 1). The approvals must come from N distinct eligible approvers.
136	  - N is fixed for a mission when it's submitted. Later changes to the setting don't affect a round already in progress.
137	  - An approval already given still counts if that Director is later deactivated or demoted.
138	- **A2** Leads can't approve anything, which satisfies R3. **[B]** A Director can't approve a mission they created or are rostered on.
139	- **A3** Each Director approves a round at most once. A single rejection ends the round.
140	
141	### 5.6 Assignments
142	
143	```
144	Proposed ──accept──▶ Accepted ──withdraw (reason)──▶ Withdrawn
145	   ├──decline──▶ Declined   └──removed by Lead──▶ Removed
146	   └──retracted by Lead──▶ Removed
147	Accepted ──mission completed──▶ Completed;  open ──mission cancelled──▶ Cancelled
148	```
149	
150	- **S1** The owning Lead can propose only eligible crew. A person can hold at most one open assignment per mission. A slot can't have more open assignments than its headcount. A Lead can propose themselves, but they must be eligible and must accept like anyone else.
151	- **S2** Crew can accept only while the mission is in Draft and they're still eligible. Accepted assignments block overlapping dates. Proposals don't block anything.
152	- **S3** Declining takes an optional reason. Withdrawing needs a reason and is allowed only in Draft or Pending Approval.
153	- **S4** Every assignment needs the crew member's acceptance. Crew can't be placed on a mission directly.
154	
155	### 5.7 Visibility and audit
156	
157	| | Crew | Lead | Director |
158	|---|---|---|---|
159	| Own profile, skills, availability, assignments | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
160	| Missions they're on, with teammates' names and slots | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
161	| Other crew members' profiles, skills, busy/free dates | ✗ | ✓ | ✓ |
162	| Other people's *reasons* for unavailability | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ |
163	| All missions in the organisation | ✗ | read (edit own) | read |
164	| Users, catalogue management, organisation settings | ✗ | read catalogue | manage |
165	
166	- **V1** Every state change, approval, rejection and assignment response is recorded with who did it, when, and any reason. Anyone who can see the mission can see this history.
167	- **V2** Former assignees see a summary of the mission in their own assignment history. They don't get full access to the mission unless their role allows it. Former assignees are people whose assignment was declined, withdrawn, removed or cancelled.
168	
169	## 6. Auto-matching
170	
171	The matcher only advises. It suggests crew for a Draft mission's open places and changes nothing. The owner or a Director can preview a suggestion. When the owning Lead applies it, it creates Proposed assignments, which crew still have to accept.
172	
173	**Candidates.** Active crew profiles in the mission's organisation only. Crew who already have an open assignment on this mission, or who declined or withdrew from it, are left out.
174	
175	**Workload.** The total mission-days of a crew member's accepted assignments on missions that are not Completed, leaving out the mission being matched.
176	
177	**Algorithm**
178	
179	1. **Filter.** For each slot with open places, keep the candidates who are eligible (§5.1).
180	2. **Pick the most constrained slot.** Choose the slot with the fewest remaining eligible candidates. Ties go to the slot listed first.
181	3. **Fill one place in that slot.** Rank candidates by:
182	   1. lowest workload;
183	   2. then highest proficiency, meaning the sum of their levels across the slot's required skills;
184	   3. then stable identifier, ascending.
185	4. **Remove the chosen person** from every other slot's candidates.
186	5. **Repeat** from step 2 until every place is filled or has no candidates left.
187	
188	**Output.** A suggested roster with no one in two slots. Places without a candidate are marked **unfilled**. The same inputs always give the same output.
189	
190	**Why:**
191	- Filling the most constrained slot first stops a versatile person from being spent on a slot that's easy to fill.
192	- Ranking by lowest workload spreads missions across crew.
193	- Proficiency only breaks ties, because everyone left already meets the requirement.
194	
195	**Known limitation:** this is a greedy heuristic. It can occasionally leave a place unfilled when a complete roster exists. That's acceptable for an advisory tool, and the Lead can still propose by hand.
196	
197	## 7. CLI requirements
198	
199	- **C1** Every workflow in §4 can be carried out from the CLI. **[B]**
200	- **C2** Refusals explain why in domain terms, for example "Gus has an accepted assignment on *Station Reboost* overlapping 1–2 Mar". Where possible they also say what to do next.
201	- **C3** Output is readable by people by default, with a machine-readable option for scripts and tests.
202	- **C4** Actions that send a mission back to Draft, or cancel it, warn before running.
203	
204	## 8. Acceptance criteria
205	
206	Each criterion starts from a fresh seed. Scenario numbers (S-) refer to §9.3.
207	
208	**Mission workflow**
209	
210	- **AC-1** The golden path (S1) ends Completed, and every transition appears in the history with who did it and when.
211	- **AC-2** Submission is refused while any place is unfilled or any proposal is unanswered. The refusal lists them.
212	- **AC-3** Approvals before the N-th leave the mission Pending. The N-th approval makes it Active.
213	- **AC-4** Changing the dates, slots or roster of a Pending mission sends it back to Draft and clears its approvals. So does a crew withdrawal (S5).
214	- **AC-5** On an Active mission, edits, proposals, removals and withdrawals are refused. Completion and cancellation succeed.
215	- **AC-6** Cancelling needs a reason. The owning Lead or any Director can cancel, and any other Lead is refused. Open assignments become Cancelled.
216	- **AC-7** Only the owning Lead can edit, propose, apply or submit. After a Director reassigns the owner, the new owner can and the previous owner can't.
217	
218	**Approval restrictions**
219	
220	- **AC-8** The mission's creator can't approve it. In S1, Ruth is refused.
221	- **AC-9** Leads and Crew can't approve or reject.
222	- **AC-10** A Director can't approve the same round twice. Rejecting needs a reason and clears earlier approvals (S6).
223	- **AC-11** A Director rostered on a mission can't approve it.
224	- **AC-12** Submission is refused when there are fewer than N eligible approvers, not counting the creator or rostered Directors (S7).
225	- **AC-13** In Helix (N = 1), a single approval makes the mission Active (S8).
226	
227	**Overlaps and availability**
228	
229	- **AC-14** Proposing someone who is unavailable, or who has an overlapping accepted assignment, is refused. In S4, both Fay and Gus are refused.
230	- **AC-15** Overlapping proposals are allowed. Accepting one that overlaps an accepted assignment is refused, and the refusal names the conflicting mission (S4, Chen).
231	- **AC-16** Recording unavailability that overlaps an accepted assignment is refused.
232	- **AC-17** Two ranges that share only a boundary day overlap.
233	
234	**Matching**
235	
236	- **AC-18** S2 gives exactly the expected roster on every run.
237	- **AC-19** S3 marks Geologist as unfilled. S3b shows a new accepted assignment changing the suggestion.
238	- **AC-20** Workload counts only accepted assignments on missions that aren't Completed. Chen's workload is 5, not 35.
239	- **AC-21** The suggestion never puts one person in two slots. It never includes anyone who is ineligible, from another organisation, or already excluded from this mission.
240	- **AC-22** Running the matcher changes nothing. Applying it creates Proposed assignments.
241	
242	**Roles and visibility**
243	
244	- **AC-23** Ana can't change her own skill levels, but Lee can change hers. Ben can't edit skills, but he can edit his personal details and unavailability.
245	- **AC-24** Lee sees Fay as busy 5–8 Mar but doesn't see the reason. Ruth sees the reason. Ben can't view Fay's profile, and his mission list shows only missions he's on.
246	- **AC-25** Leads can't manage users, the catalogue or the approval setting.
247	- **AC-26** Deactivating someone with open assignments is refused. A deactivated user can't sign in. The last active Director can't be deactivated or demoted.
248	
249	**Tenant isolation (S9)**
250	
251	- **AC-27** Requesting another organisation's mission, crew member, user, skill or assignment by ID gives the same response as an ID that doesn't exist.
252	- **AC-28** Every list contains only the caller's organisation's records.
253	- **AC-29** Any write that references another organisation's ID fails as "not found", and neither organisation's data changes.
254	- **AC-30** Approving or rejecting another organisation's mission fails as "not found".
255	- **AC-31** Helix's Kai (Piloting 5, no commitments) is never suggested for an Aurora mission.
256

311	
312	## 10. Test plan
313	
314	Every acceptance criterion maps to at least one automated test.
315	
316	| Level | Covers |
317	|---|---|
318	| Unit: matcher (pure function) | Eligibility, workload, most-constrained-first, each tie-break, no person in two slots, unfilled places, determinism. Includes S2, S3 and a small hand-built case per rule. |
319	| Unit: lifecycle and rules | Every allowed transition and a sample of disallowed ones. Approver eligibility and the N count. Revert-to-Draft clearing approvals. The Active freeze. Overlap at boundary days. |
320	| API: permissions | A role × action table, checking each role is allowed or refused as expected. |
321	| API: tenant isolation | Every endpoint, for reads, lists and writes, tried with the other organisation's IDs. A route-inventory test makes any new endpoint fail CI until it's added to this sweep. |
322	| API: scenarios | S1 and S3b–S8 against a freshly seeded database. |
323	| CLI end to end | S1 and S4 run through the real CLI, checking machine-readable output and the wording of refusals. |
324	
325	## 11. Out of scope
326	
327	- Web UI.
328	- Self-service organisation signup, users in more than one organisation, SSO.
329	- Password recovery and email flows. Deleting crew profiles. Reactivating deactivated users.
330	- Delivering notifications. The CLI's "needs attention" views replace them.
331	- Certifications, skill expiry, skill verification, skill hierarchies.
332	- Recurring availability, scheduling finer than a day, rest periods between missions.
333	- Conditional or multi-stage approvals.
334	- Assigning crew without their acceptance, response deadlines.
335	- Matcher extras: preferred skills, experience scoring, configurable weights, near-miss and exclusion lists, suggestions to relax requirements, LLM matching.
336	- Date-driven transitions. Roster changes once a mission is Active.
337	- Equipment and resource constraints. Keep slot requirements extensible so these can be added later.
338	- Billing, bulk import.
339
````

4	>
5	> The PRD defines *what* the system does. This document defines *how* we build it, *why* we chose this approach, and *in what order* we deliver it. IDs such as P3, M7 or AC-15 refer to the PRD.

7	**Contents**

22	---
23	
24	## 1. Summary
25	
26	**What we're building.** A multi-tenant API and CLI for planning space missions:
27	- organisations manage crew and their skills;
28	- Leads define missions and get crew suggestions from a deterministic matcher;
29	- Directors approve missions before they go active.
30	
31	**Design principles**
32	
33	1. **Business rules live in one place.** All rules are pure functions in `domain/`, so they're easy to test and shared by every entry point.
34	2. **Tenant isolation in depth.** Identity, queries, database constraints and tests each block cross-tenant access independently.
35	3. **The CLI is just a client.** It uses the same API as any other client, so the API is the only place behaviour is enforced.
36	4. **Simple to run, simple to reason about.** One process, one SQLite file, no background jobs.
37	5. **Test-first.** Every module begins with failing tests derived from PRD acceptance criteria.
38	
39	**Non-goals for v1:** web UI, horizontal scaling, notifications, background scheduling, SSO, migrations tooling. See [Appendix B](#appendix-b-trade-offs-and-considerations) for the trade-offs and what we'd change at scale.
40	
41	---
42	
43	## 2. Architecture
44	
45	**Stack:** Python 3.12 · FastAPI + Pydantic v2 · SQLite through stdlib `sqlite3` · argon2-cffi · Typer + httpx2 · uv, pytest, ruff
46	
47	**Dependency policy:** don't use `httpx` anywhere, including tests. Use `httpx2`, which Starlette's `TestClient` now expects.
48	
49	Why SQLite, hand-written SQL, and a CLI that goes through the API: [TR-1](#tr-1), [TR-2](#tr-2), [TR-4](#tr-4).
50	
51	```mermaid
52	flowchart TB
53	    U([Users]) --> CLI[CLI]
54	    CLI -->|HTTP + JSON| API[API]
55	    API --> S[Services]
56	    S --> D[Domain rules]
57	    S --> R[Repositories]
58	    R --> DB[(SQLite)]
59	    A[mc-admin] --> DB
60	```
61	
62	| Layer | Folder | Responsibility | May depend on |
63	|---|---|---|---|
64	| CLI | `cli/` | Commands, output, prompts | API over HTTP only |
65	| API | `api/` | Validate input, resolve `Actor`, call a service, map errors | services |
66	| Services | `services/` | One use case = one transaction + audit | domain, infra |
67	| Domain | `domain/` | Every business rule, pure (no I/O) | nothing |
68	| Infra | `infra/` | Connections, schema, scoped repositories, security | domain types |
69	
70	**How a request flows** (example: `mc missions submit 12`)
71	
72	1. The CLI sends `POST /api/v1/missions/12/submit` with the bearer token.
73	2. The API resolves the token to an `Actor` (user, organisation, role) and calls `services.missions.submit(actor, 12)`.
74	3. The service opens `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and loads the mission through `Repos(conn, actor.org_id)`.
75	4. `domain.lifecycle.check_submit(...)` returns a list of problems. If the list is empty, the service writes the new status and an audit event, then commits.
76	5. The API returns `200` with the mission, or `409` with every problem. The CLI prints one bullet per problem and exits with code `1`.
77	
78	<details><summary>Folder layout</summary>
79	
80	```
81	src/mission_control/
82	  domain/    dates  enums  errors  eligibility  matching  lifecycle  approvals  permissions  passwords  records
83	  infra/     db  schema.sql  security  repositories/
84	  services/  auth  admin  crew  missions  assignments  approvals  matching  audit  inbox
85	  api/       app  deps  errors  schemas/  routers/
86	  cli/       main  client  config  render  resolve  commands/
87	  seed/      data  loader
88	  admin_cli.py        # mc-admin: init-db, seed, create-org
89	tests/  unit/  integration/  api/  cli/  builders.py  conftest.py
90	```
91	</details>
92	
93	---
94	
95	## 3. Data model
96	
97	The core entities are below. Skills, unavailability, approvals, tokens and the audit log hang off these. All 13 tables are in [Appendix A](#appendix-a-schema-sql).
98	
99	```mermaid
100	flowchart LR
101	    Org[Organisation] --> User
102	    Org --> Mission
103	    User --> Crew[Crew profile]
104	    Mission --> Slot
105	    Slot --> Assignment
106	    Crew --> Assignment
107	```
108	
109	**Tenant safety.** Every tenant table has `org_id` and `UNIQUE (org_id, id)`. Every reference between tenant rows includes `org_id`, so the database itself rejects links across organisations. Why a shared database: [TR-3](#tr-3).
110	
111	| Enforced by the database | Enforced by services |
112	|---|---|
113	| Value ranges and valid dates | Eligibility and overlaps |
114	| Cross-organisation links rejected | Open places |
115	| One open assignment per person per mission | State transitions |
116	| A slot belongs to its mission | Permissions and the owner's role |
117	| Reasons for withdrawals and rejections | At least one requirement per slot |
118	| Append-only audit log; used skills can't be deleted | |
119	
120	**Modelling choices**
121	- **Approval rounds.** Submit sets `round += 1` and saves N in `approvals_required`. Only approvals in the current round count, and none are ever deleted ([TR-9](#tr-9)).
122	- **Soft-deleted slots.** `removed_at` keeps the history of past assignments.
123	- **Nothing derived is stored.** Workload, eligibility, open places and the roster are computed when needed ([TR-7](#tr-7)).
124	- **Integer IDs** keep CLI commands short ([TR-12](#tr-12)).
125	- **History survives renames.** Audit `data` keeps the names that applied at the time.
126	- **"On a mission"** means holding an open or completed assignment. Any other status gives summary access only (V2).
127	
128	---
129	
130	## 4. Security and tenancy
131	
132	### Authentication
133	
134	| | |
135	|---|---|
136	| Token | 32 random bytes. Only the SHA-256 hash is stored. 12-hour expiry. |
137	| Every request | Token → valid? → user active? → `Actor(user_id, org_id, role, crew_profile_id)`. Nothing is cached, so role changes apply immediately. |
138	| Revocation | Logout revokes that token. Deactivation or a password change revokes every token. Opaque tokens make this immediate ([TR-5](#tr-5)). |
139	| Passwords | Argon2id. At least 15 characters. No character-type rules. |
140	
141	### Permissions
142	
143	Each **role** sets the baseline:
144	
145	| Role | Can | Can't |
146	|---|---|---|
147	| **Director** | Run the organisation: users, roles, skills, approval setting. See everything. Approve, transfer, complete and cancel missions. | Edit mission details, since that's the owner's job |
148	| **Lead** | Create and run their own missions. Rate other people's skills. See all crew and missions. | Approve anything. Touch missions they don't own. |
149	| **Crew** | Manage their own profile and availability. Respond to their own assignments. | See other crew, or missions they aren't on |
150	
151	On top of the role, **relationship rules** make the decisions that matter:
152	
153	| Rule | Effect | PRD |
154	|---|---|---|
155	| **Owner only** | Only the mission's owner edits it, proposes crew, applies the matcher or submits | M3 |
156	| **Not yourself** | Nobody sets their own skill levels | P3 |
157	| **Eligible approver** | A Director can't approve a mission they created or are on | A2 |
158	| **Self only** | Only you edit your details and availability. Only you, and Directors, see your unavailability reasons. | P4, §5.7 |
159	| **Visibility** | Crew only see missions they're on. Former assignees see a summary. | V2 |
160	
161	**Enforcement.** `domain/permissions.authorize(actor, action, ctx)` is the single entry point. The complete action × role table is the parametrized test for this module, and that test is the source of truth.
162	
163	| Status | When |
164	|---|---|
165	| **401** | Authentication failed |
166	| **404** | Missing, another organisation's, or not visible. All three look identical ([TR-10](#tr-10)). |
167	| **403** | Visible, but you're not allowed to do this |
168	
169	### Tenant isolation
170	
171	1. **Identity.** `org_id` comes only from the token, never from the request.
172	2. **Repositories.** `Repos(conn, org_id)` scopes every query. Another organisation's ID behaves as missing.
173	3. **Database.** Composite `(org_id, id)` foreign keys.
174	4. **Tests.**
175	   - An SQL lint checks every query mentions `org_id`.
176	   - A route inventory makes sure no endpoint is left out of the isolation tests.
177	   - A cross-organisation sweep compares database snapshots before and after.
178	
179	---
180	
181	## 5. Lifecycle
182	
183	### Mission
184	
185	```mermaid
186	stateDiagram-v2
187	    direction LR
188	    Draft --> Pending: submit
189	    Pending --> Active: final approval
190	    Pending --> Draft: reject / change
191	    Active --> Completed: complete
192	```
193	
194	Any state before Completed can also be **cancelled**.
195	
196	| Action | Who | Must be true | Effect |
197	|---|---|---|---|
198	| submit | owner | Slots full, all accepted, roster eligible, eligible approvers ≥ N | `round += 1`, N saved |
199	| approve | eligible Director | Hasn't decided this round. Roster eligible. | Active once N is reached. **If a check fails, nothing is written.** |
200	| reject | eligible Director | Reason given | → Draft |
201	| transfer | Director | New owner is an active Lead | Pending → Draft. Active stays Active. |
202	| complete | owner or Director | Mission is Active | Accepted assignments → completed |
203	| cancel | owner or Director | Reason given | Open assignments → cancelled |
204	
205	### Assignment
206	
207	```mermaid
208	stateDiagram-v2
209	    direction LR
210	    Proposed --> Accepted: accept
211	    Proposed --> Declined: decline
212	    Accepted --> Withdrawn: withdraw
213	    Accepted --> Completed: mission completed
214	```
215	
216	The owner can also **remove** an open assignment. Cancelling the mission **cancels** every open assignment.
217	
218	| Action | Who | Mission must be | Notes |
219	|---|---|---|---|
220	| propose | owner | Draft or Pending* | Person eligible, place open, not already on this mission |
221	| accept | crew member | Draft | Still eligible. An overlap names the conflicting mission. |
222	| decline | crew member | Draft | Reason optional |
223	| withdraw | crew member | Draft or Pending* | Reason required |
224	| remove | owner | Draft or Pending* | |
225	
226	\* Sends a Pending mission back to Draft.
227	
228	### Cross-cutting rules
229	
230	| Rule | How it's implemented |
231	|---|---|
232	| **Back to Draft** | Every mutating service calls `touch_for_change()` first. Draft → no change. Pending → Draft. Active → `mission_frozen`. Final → `mission_final`. |
233	| **Edits can't break assignments (M7)** | `invalidated_by()` checks slot removal, headcount, requirements and dates → a `rule_violation` naming everyone affected |
234	| **Skill changes (P3)** | Allowed. Refused only if they'd break an **Active** assignment → `invalidates_active_assignment` |
235	| **Audit** | Written in the same transaction as the change. Refused actions write nothing. |
236	| **Transactions** | One connection per request. `foreign_keys=ON`. `busy_timeout=5000`. `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` *before* checks. Roll back on error. Lock timeout → 503 `busy`. ([TR-11](#tr-11)) |
237	
238	---
239	
240	## 6. Matching
241	
242	The matcher suggests crew for a Draft mission's open places. It's a pure function: same inputs, same output. It's greedy rather than optimal, which keeps it explainable ([TR-6](#tr-6)).
243	
244	**How it works**
245	
246	1. **Filter.** For each slot, keep candidates who are eligible: active, skills meet the requirements, available, and no overlapping accepted assignment.
247	2. **Pick a slot.** Take the open slot with the fewest candidates, so scarce skills are protected.
248	3. **Pick a person.** Take the lowest workload, then the highest proficiency, then the lowest ID.
249	4. **Place** that person and remove them from every other slot's candidates.
250	5. **Repeat** until all places are filled or marked **unfilled**.
251	
252	**Inputs** (`domain/records.py`). They carry names as well as IDs, so every refusal can name the person, skill and mission involved:
253	
254	| Record | Fields |
255	|---|---|
256	| `Requirement` | `skill_id`, `skill_name`, `min_level` |
257	| `SlotSpec` | `id`, `position`, `name`, `open_places`, `requirements: tuple[Requirement, ...]` |
258	| `Commitment` | `mission_id`, `mission_name`, `window`: an accepted assignment on another non-final mission |
259	| `CandidateFacts` | `crew_profile_id`, `name`, `active`, `skills: {skill_id: level}`, `unavailable: tuple[DateRange, ...]`, `accepted_elsewhere: tuple[Commitment, ...]`, `workload` |
260	| `RosterEntry` | `assignment_id`, `slot: SlotSpec`, `crew: CandidateFacts`, `status`: an open assignment on the mission being edited or submitted (`crew.accepted_elsewhere` excludes this mission) |
261	| `AssignmentFact` | `mission_id`, `mission_status`, `assignment_status`, `window`: one of a person's assignments, used to compute workload |
262	| `ActiveAssignment` | `mission_id`, `mission_name`, `slot_name`, `requirements`: an accepted assignment on an Active mission, used to check skill changes |
263	
264	```python
265	def suggest_roster(slots: list[SlotSpec], candidates: list[CandidateFacts],
266	                   window: DateRange) -> Suggestion:
267	    by_id = {c.crew_profile_id: c for c in candidates}
268	    eligible = {s.id: {c.crew_profile_id for c in candidates if is_eligible(c, s, window)}
269	                for s in slots}
270	    remaining = {s.id: s.open_places for s in slots}
271	    placements, unfilled = [], []
272	
273	    while open_slots := [s for s in slots if remaining[s.id] > 0]:
274	        # Most-constrained slot first; ties by listed position, then id.
275	        slot = min(open_slots, key=lambda s: (len(eligible[s.id]), s.position, s.id))
276	
277	        if not eligible[slot.id]:
278	            unfilled.append(Unfilled(slot.id, remaining[slot.id]))
279	            remaining[slot.id] = 0
280	            continue
281	
282	        # Lowest workload, then highest proficiency, then stable id.
283	        person = min((by_id[i] for i in eligible[slot.id]),
284	                     key=lambda c: (c.workload, -proficiency(c, slot), c.crew_profile_id))
285	
286	        placements.append(Placement(slot.id, person.crew_profile_id))
287	        remaining[slot.id] -= 1
288	        for ids in eligible.values():
289	            ids.discard(person.crew_profile_id)
290	
291	    return Suggestion(placements, unfilled)
292	
293	
294	def proficiency(c: CandidateFacts, slot: SlotSpec) -> int:
295	    return sum(c.skills.get(r.skill_id, 0) for r in slot.requirements)
296	```
297	
298	**Workload** = total mission-days of accepted assignments on missions that aren't completed, leaving out the mission being matched.
299	
300	| | Preview | Apply |
301	|---|---|---|
302	| Who | Owner or Director | Owner only |
303	| Writes | Nothing | Proposals, created through the normal `propose` path |
304	| Data | Loaded with one query per fact type | Recomputed inside the write transaction, so it's never stale |
305	
306	---
307	
308	## 7. API
309	
310	**Conventions**
311	- REST and JSON, under `/api/v1`.
312	- Authentication with the header `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
313	- Lifecycle changes are explicit `POST` commands, for example `/submit`. They are never `PATCH status`, because each one has its own guards and audit event ([TR-8](#tr-8)).
314	- List endpoints return only what the caller is allowed to see.
315	
316	#### Auth and me
317	| Method | Path | Purpose |
318	|---|---|---|
319	| POST | `/auth/login` | Exchange email and password for a token |
320	| POST | `/auth/logout` | Revoke the current token |
321	| GET | `/me` | Current user, role and crew profile |
322	| POST | `/me/password` | Change password (revokes all sessions) |
323	| GET | `/me/inbox` | Things waiting on me: a to-do list computed from current data (see below) |
324	
325	#### Organisation, users and skills (Directors)
326	| Method | Path | Purpose |
327	|---|---|---|
328	| GET · PATCH | `/org` | View settings, or set the required approvals |
329	| GET · POST | `/users` | List users, or create one with an initial password |
330	| PATCH | `/users/{id}` | Change role or deactivate |
331	| POST | `/users/{id}/crew-profile` | Give a user a crew profile |
332	| GET · POST | `/skills` | List skills (Leads too), or add one |
333	| PATCH · DELETE | `/skills/{id}` | Rename, or delete if unused |
334	
335	#### Crew
336	| Method | Path | Purpose |
337	|---|---|---|
338	| GET | `/crew` · `/crew/{id}` | List or view profiles |
339	| PATCH | `/crew/{id}` | Edit own display name and bio |
340	| PUT · DELETE | `/crew/{id}/skills/{skill_id}` | Set or remove a skill level |
341	| GET · POST | `/crew/{id}/unavailability` | List or add own unavailability |
342	| DELETE | `/crew/{id}/unavailability/{uid}` | Remove own unavailability |
343	| GET | `/crew/{id}/assignments` | Assignment history (summaries) |
344	
345	#### Missions
346	| Method | Path | Purpose |
347	|---|---|---|
348	| GET · POST | `/missions` | List visible missions, or create one with slots |
349	| GET · PATCH | `/missions/{id}` | View, or edit name and dates |
350	| POST | `/missions/{id}/slots` | Add a slot |
351	| PATCH · DELETE | `/missions/{id}/slots/{sid}` | Edit or remove a slot |
352	| GET | `/missions/{id}/history` | Audit trail |
353	| POST | `/missions/{id}/submit` · `approve` · `reject` · `complete` · `cancel` | Lifecycle commands |
354	| POST | `/missions/{id}/owner` | Transfer ownership |
355	| GET | `/missions/{id}/match` | Preview suggested roster |
356	| POST | `/missions/{id}/match/apply` | Create proposals from the suggestion |
357	
358	#### Assignments
359	| Method | Path | Purpose |
360	|---|---|---|
361	| POST | `/missions/{id}/assignments` | Propose a crew member for a slot |
362	| GET | `/assignments` | My assignments |
363	| POST | `/assignments/{id}/accept` · `decline` · `withdraw` · `remove` | Respond or remove |
364	
365	#### Inbox
366	
367	`GET /me/inbox` (`mc inbox`) lists only what is waiting on the caller to act. It's computed from current data on each request, so nothing is stored, nothing is marked as read, and an item disappears once it's dealt with. Every role has one inbox, showing whichever sections apply:
368	
369	| Section | Shown to | Item | Action |
370	|---|---|---|---|
371	| Proposals | Anyone with a crew profile | Their own Proposed assignments on Draft missions | accept or decline |
372	| Approvals | Directors | Pending missions they're an eligible approver for and haven't approved this round | approve or reject |
373	| Missions | The owning Lead | Their Draft missions, each with what's waiting: places to fill (open places, including after a decline, withdrawal or rejection), proposals awaiting replies, or "ready to submit" | staff or submit |
374	
375	Not shown: pending missions waiting on others, Active missions, and anything informational. Each section is a few indexed lookups of the caller's own rows (`assignments_profile`, `missions_org_status`, `missions_owner`); the missions section shows counts only and leaves the full eligibility check to submit.
376	
377	#### Errors
378	
379	Every error has the same shape, for every status code (401, 403, 404, 409, 422, 503). `problems` is always present and always has at least one entry, so clients only ever handle one format. A rule or state conflict (409) lists **every** problem at once. A rule refusal's code is always `rule_violation`, with a summary message ("Ben can't be proposed as Flight Engineer on Lunar Gateway Resupply"); each reason is a problem with its own code. Only a state conflict has a code of its own (one of the **State** codes below), repeated as its single problem. A permission refusal (403) lists its single reason, because only one applies and the actor can't fix it themselves. Each problem names the record involved:
380	
381	```json
382	{
383	  "error": {
384	    "code": "rule_violation",
385	    "message": "Mission can't be submitted",
386	    "problems": [
387	      { "code": "slot_unfilled", "message": "Commander: 1 of 1 place unfilled", "refs": { "slot_id": 12 } }
388	    ]
389	  }
390	}
391	```
392	
393	| HTTP | Meaning | Codes |
394	|---|---|---|
395	| 401 | Not authenticated | `invalid_credentials` `unauthenticated` |
396	| 403 | Not allowed | `role_forbidden` `not_owner` `approver_is_creator` `approver_is_rostered` `self_skill_edit` `wrong_current_password` |
397	| 404 | Not found or not visible | `not_found` |
398	| 409 | Conflicts with state or a rule | **State:** `mission_frozen` `mission_final` `invalid_transition` `already_decided`<br>**Rules:** `rule_violation`, with the reasons as problem codes (below) |
399	| 422 | Invalid input | `validation_error` `password_too_short` |
400	| 503 | Database busy, retry | `busy` |
401	
402	Malformed mission and slot values are 422 `validation_error`, and every mistake is listed at once: a blank mission or slot name, a headcount below 1, a slot with no required skill, a minimum level outside 1–5, an end date before the start date, or a Director creating a mission without naming its owner.
403	
404	**Problem codes.** These appear inside `problems` and explain each refusal. Adding one means updating this table in the same change.
405	
406	| Code | Raised when | Typical `refs` |
407	|---|---|---|
408	| `inactive` | The person is deactivated | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
409	| `skill_below_minimum` | A required skill is missing or below the minimum | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id`, `skill_id` |
410	| `unavailable` | Unavailability overlaps the mission's dates | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
411	| `overlap` | An accepted assignment on another mission overlaps (the message names that mission) | `crew_profile_id`, `mission_id`, `mission_name` |
412	| `slot_has_open_assignments` | Removing a slot that still has crew | `assignment_id`, `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
413	| `headcount_below_open_assignments` | Lowering headcount below the assigned crew (the message names them) | `slot_id`, `crew_profile_ids` |
414	| `invalidates_active_assignment` | A skill change would break an assignment on an Active mission | `mission_id`, `skill_id` |
415	| `slot_unfilled` | Submitting with empty places | `slot_id` |
416	| `proposal_unanswered` | Submitting while a proposal is still waiting for a reply (the message names the person and slot) | `assignment_id`, `slot_id`, `crew_profile_id` |
417	| `already_exists` | Inside a 409 `rule_violation`: the email address is already in use (anywhere on the platform, since login is by email alone; see [TR-13](#tr-13)), a skill name is already in the catalogue (ignoring case), the user already has a crew profile, or a mission already has a live slot with that name (ignoring case; a new mission's slots are also checked against each other, and have no `slot_id` yet) | `email`; `skill_id`; `user_id`, `crew_profile_id`; `slot_id` |
418	| `owner_not_active_lead` | The owner named for a new mission, or for a transfer, isn't a Mission Lead or is deactivated (inside a 409 `rule_violation`) | `user_id` |
419	| `insufficient_approvers` | Submitting when fewer eligible approvers exist than the approvals required | — |
420	| `has_open_assignments` | Deactivating someone who still holds proposed or accepted assignments | `user_id`, `mission_ids` |
421	| `owns_open_missions` | Deactivating, or changing the role of, a Lead who owns unfinished missions | `user_id`, `mission_ids` |
422	| `last_director` | Deactivating or demoting the organisation's only active Director | `user_id` |
423	| `skill_in_use` | Deleting a skill that crew hold or slots require (the message names them) | `skill_id`, `crew_profile_ids`, `mission_ids` |
424	| `mission_needs_a_slot` | Removing a mission's only slot | `slot_id` |
425	| `already_owner` | Transferring a mission to the Lead who already owns it | `user_id` |
426	| `slot_full` | Proposing someone for a slot with no open places (the message names the slot and mission) | `slot_id` |
427	| `already_assigned` | Proposing someone who already holds an open assignment on this mission (the message names their slot) | `assignment_id`, `crew_profile_id`, `mission_id` |
428

430	
431	## 8. CLI
432	
433	| Concern | Behaviour |
434	|---|---|
435	| Client | An `httpx2` wrapper passed into commands, so tests use `TestClient` and need no server |
436	| Auth | Token stored in `~/.config/mission-control/credentials.json` (mode 0600). Cleared on logout, password change or 401. |
437	| References | Missions and assignments by ID. People by email. Skills by name. Slots by name or position. |
438	| Passwords | Hidden prompt only, never a flag. Asked twice when setting one. |
439	| Output | Plain tables. No meaning carried by colour alone. `--json` for scripts. |
440	| Errors | The message, then one bullet per problem, then a hint for known codes |
441	| Exit codes | `0` OK · `1` refused · `2` usage · `3` authentication · `4` unreachable or busy |
442	| Confirmations | Changing a Pending mission, or cancelling, asks first. `--yes` skips the prompt. |
443	
444	```bash
445	# Everyone
446	mc login | logout | whoami | password | inbox
447	mc me show | update | unavailable add|list|remove
448	mc assignments list [--pending] | accept ID | decline ID | withdraw ID --reason R
449	
450	# Leads
451	mc missions create --name N --start D --end D --slot "Commander:1:Piloting>=4,Leadership>=3"
452	mc missions edit | slot add|edit|remove | match ID [--apply]
453	mc missions propose ID --slot S --crew EMAIL | remove-crew ID ASSIGNMENT | submit ID
454	mc crew list | show | history | set-skill EMAIL SKILL LEVEL | unset-skill EMAIL SKILL
455	
456	# Directors
457	mc missions approve ID | reject ID --reason R | transfer ID --owner EMAIL
458	mc missions complete ID | cancel ID --reason R | list | show | history
459	mc users list | add | set-role | deactivate | enable-crew
460	mc skills list | add | rename | remove          mc org show | set-approvals N
461	```

465	## 9. Delivery plan
466	
467	We deliver in four phases. Each phase ends in something that can be demonstrated and tested.
468	
469	```mermaid
470	flowchart LR
471	    A[1 Pure domain<br/>M0–M5] --> B[2 Persistence<br/>M6–M8] --> C[3 Services<br/>M9–M14] --> D[4 Interfaces<br/>M15–M17]
472	```
473	
474	| Phase | Milestone (what you can show) |
475	|---|---|
476	| 1. Pure domain | Matcher reproduces S2 and S3; lifecycle and permission tables pass, with no database involved |
477	| 2. Persistence | Seeded database matches PRD §9; tenant-scoped queries; login works |
478	| 3. Services | Every scenario passes at the service level (S1, S3b–S8) |
479	| 4. Interfaces | Full golden path (S1) through the real CLI; isolation sweep passes; README smoke test passes |
480	
481	| # | Module | Write these tests first |
482	|---|---|---|
483	| **Phase 1** | | |
484	| M0 | Skeleton | `/health` returns 200 |
485	| M1 | dates | Boundary-day overlap (AC-17) · mission-days · end ≥ start |
486	| M2 | eligibility, passwords | Each ineligibility reason · workload (AC-20) · M7 and P3 checks · 14 characters rejected, 15 accepted |
487	| M3 | matching | S2, S3 · each tie-break · most-constrained first · unfilled · determinism |
488	| M4 | lifecycle, approvals | All submit problems at once (AC-2) · quorum uses saved N · approver rules (AC-8, 11, 12) · failed approval writes nothing |
489	| M5 | permissions | Full action × role table |
490	| **Phase 2** | | |
491	| M6 | schema | Pragmas · rollback · constraints · composite foreign keys |
492	| M7 | repos, seed | Seed matches PRD §9 · scoped `get` · V2 visibility · SQL lint |
493	| M8 | auth | Login · inactive user · expiry · every revocation path · role read fresh |
494	| **Phase 3** | | |
495	| SF | service foundation | Service context (actor + `Repos` + transaction + audit) · `RuleViolation` carrying problems · refused view becomes not-found · fact loaders building `CandidateFacts` / `RosterEntry` / `AssignmentFact` / `ActiveAssignment` from the database · the repository writes Wave 1 needs |
496	| M9 | admin | P1, P7–P10 · last Director (AC-25, 26) |
497	| M10 | crew | Self-only edits (AC-16) · skill rules (AC-23) · reasons hidden (AC-24) |
498	| M11 | missions | M7 · back to Draft (AC-4) · Active freeze · transfer (AC-7) |
499	| M12 | assignments | Propose (AC-14) · overlap on accept (AC-15) · withdraw (S5) · cascades |
500	| M13 | approvals | S1, S6, S7, S8 · audit trail (AC-1) |
501	| M14 | matching service | Preview writes nothing (AC-22) · apply · isolation (AC-31) · S3b |
502	| **Phase 4** | | |
503	| M15 | API | Error mapping: **every** error body has a non-empty `problems` list, 403/404/401/422/503 included · permissions over HTTP · isolation sweep (S9) · scenarios |
504	| M16 | CLI | Reference resolution · `--slot` parser · prompts · exit codes · S1, S4 |
505	| M17 | README | Smoke-test the README commands |
506	
507	**Waves.** Independent modules may be built at the same time, each in its own git worktree ([§10](#working-in-waves-worktrees)), and then reviewed and committed one at a time. Phase 3 runs as:
508	
509	```
510	SF service foundation (on main)
511	  → Wave 1: M9 admin ∥ M10 crew ∥ M11 missions
512	  → M12 assignments
513	  → Wave 3: M13 approvals ∥ M14 matching service
514	```
515	
516	SF comes first so the waves share one service context and one set of fact loaders instead of each writing their own. Earlier pairs that were independent: M3 ∥ M4, M5 ∥ M6.
517	
518	**Definition of done (every module)**
519	- [ ] Tests were written first, and each test name includes the PRD ID it covers.
520	- [ ] `uv run pytest` and `uv run ruff check` both pass on the whole repo.
521	- [ ] No layer-boundary violations ([§10](#10-guidelines-for-implementing-agents)).
522	- [ ] Any new endpoint is added to the isolation sweep.
523	- [ ] The module was verified by actually running it, and the output was shown, not just the test results.
524	- [ ] A read-only review of the diff was done and its findings reported.
525	- [ ] A commit was **proposed**, and made only after the user approved it.
526	
527	---
528	
529	## 10. Guidelines for implementing agents
530	
531	### Source of truth
532	1. The **PRD** decides behaviour. **This document** decides structure. Code follows both.
533	2. **Don't invent behaviour.** If the PRD is silent or contradicts this document, stop and ask. Don't guess, and don't "improve" the rule.
534	3. Work on **one module at a time**, in the order in [§9](#9-delivery-plan). Start a module only when the user has approved it, and never before its dependencies are done.
535	
536	### The loop for each module
537	1. Read the module's row in §9 and every PRD ID it references.
538	2. Write failing tests. Name them after the rule, for example `test_ac15_accept_overlap_names_conflicting_mission`.
539	3. Write the smallest implementation that makes them pass.
540	4. Refactor, then run the **full** suite and `ruff`.
541	5. Verify the behaviour by running it for real (a REPL call, a request, or a CLI command), and show the output.
542	6. Do a read-only review of the diff, and report findings without editing anything.
543	7. Propose a commit message, such as `feat(m12): assignment propose/accept/withdraw`. **Don't commit, and don't start the next module, until the user approves.**
544	
545	### Working in waves (worktrees)
546	- **Approval:** a wave starts only when the user approves every module in it. The modules must not depend on each other.
547	- **Isolation:** each module gets its own worktree and branch, `.worktrees/<module>` on branch `<module>` (e.g. `m9-admin`), created from the current `main`. The agent works only inside it and runs `uv sync` and the tests there.
548	- **Agents don't commit.** Each leaves its changes uncommitted and reports the usual package: tests first, real run, read-only review.
549	- **Review is one branch at a time.** The user reviews only that module's diff against `main`.
550	- **Merge:** after approval, the lead commits on the branch, rebases it onto `main` if `main` has moved (re-running its tests), fast-forwards `main`, and runs the full suite on `main` before the next review.
551	- **Ports:** a real run that starts a server uses port `8000 + module number` (e.g. M11 uses 8011).
552	- **Clean-up:** remove the worktree and branch once merged.
553	
554	### Architecture rules (never break these)
555	- `domain/` imports nothing from `infra/`, `services/`, `api/` or `sqlite3`.
556	- All SQL lives in `infra/repositories/`, and every query filters on `org_id`.
557	- `org_id` comes only from the `Actor`. Never read it from request input.
558	- Every mutating service calls `touch_for_change()` first, and writes an audit event in the same transaction.
559	- Writes open `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` **before** running checks.
560	- Errors use the codes in [§7](#7-api). Adding a code means updating that table in the same change.
561	
562	### Coding conventions
563	- Type hints everywhere. Frozen dataclasses for domain records. No module-level mutable state.
564	- `date` objects in the domain, ISO strings only at the database and API edges. Timestamps are UTC.
565	- Use PRD vocabulary in names: `open_places`, `eligible_approver`, `workload`.
566	- Keep routers thin: validate, call one service, return.
567	
568	### Testing conventions
569	- Use the `seeded_db` fixture for PRD scenarios, and `tests/builders.py` for edge cases.
570	- Every test gets its own database. No network calls. No reliance on the current date: pass dates in.
571	- Never weaken or delete a test to make it pass. If a test looks wrong, raise it.
572	
573	### Stop and ask before
574	- adding a dependency;
575	- changing the schema (and if you do, update [Appendix A](#appendix-a-schema-sql) as well);
576	- adding an endpoint, error code or CLI command that isn't in this document;
577	- any case where the PRD looks ambiguous or a PRD scenario's expected outcome seems wrong.
578	
579	---
580	
581	## 11. Setup and testing
582	
583	```bash
584	uv sync
585	uv run mc-admin seed --reset      # load PRD §9 data
586	uv run mc-server                  # API on 127.0.0.1:8000
587	uv run mc login --email lee@aurora.example
588	uv run pytest
589	```
590	
591	Settings: `MC_DATABASE_PATH` · `MC_TOKEN_TTL_HOURS=12` · `MC_DB_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS=5000`
592	
593	| Level | Database | Covers |
594	|---|---|---|
595	| Unit | none | Domain rules, matcher, lifecycle, permission table |
596	| Integration | temporary file | Services, rollback, constraints, audit, SQL lint |
597	| API | temporary file | Error codes, auth, permissions, isolation sweep, scenarios S1 and S3b–S9 |
598	| CLI | temporary file | S1 and S4 end to end, `--json`, errors, exit codes, prompts |
599	
600	- **Shared seed.** `seed/data.py` drives both the demo and the tests, so they can't drift apart.
601	- **Isolated databases.** Every test gets a fresh SQLite file under `tmp_path`.
602	- **Fixtures.** `seeded_db` for scenarios; `tests/builders.py` for focused edge cases.
603	
604	---
605	
606	## Appendix A: Schema SQL
607	
608	The SQL becomes `infra/schema.sql`.
609	
610	**Conventions**
611	- Tables are `STRICT`, which needs SQLite 3.37 or later. `init_db` checks `sqlite3.sqlite_version` and refuses to start on anything older.
612	- Every tenant-owned table has `org_id NOT NULL` and `UNIQUE (org_id, id)`. That unique pair is the target for every reference from another tenant table, so a row can never point into another organisation.
613	- Dates are stored as `TEXT` in `YYYY-MM-DD` form. `date(x) IS x` rejects badly formed dates. Plain `=` wouldn't work here: it returns NULL on bad input, and a NULL check passes.
614	- Timestamps are UTC ISO `TEXT`, with the default `strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ','now')`. Below this is written as `<now>`.
615	- All foreign keys are `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (SQLite's default). The only exception is `slot_requirements`, which cascades from its slot. Domain rows are never hard-deleted except unused skills (P1) and replaced slot requirements.
616	
617	### Organisations, users and sessions
618	
619	```sql
620	PRAGMA user_version = 1;
621	
622	CREATE TABLE organisations (
623	  id                 INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
624	  name               TEXT    NOT NULL,
625	  slug               TEXT    NOT NULL UNIQUE,
626	  required_approvals INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (required_approvals >= 1),   -- A1
627	  created_at         TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>)
628	) STRICT;
629	
630	CREATE TABLE users (
631	  id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
632	  org_id        INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES organisations(id),
633	  email         TEXT    NOT NULL UNIQUE COLLATE NOCASE,        -- login is by email only
634	  name          TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (length(trim(name)) > 0),
635	  role          TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('director','lead','crew')),
636	  password_hash TEXT    NOT NULL,                               -- Argon2id encoded hash
637	  is_active     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (is_active IN (0,1)),
638	  created_at    TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
639	  updated_at    TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
640	  UNIQUE (org_id, id)
641	) STRICT;
642	CREATE INDEX users_org_role ON users(org_id, role, is_active);
643	
644	CREATE TABLE auth_tokens (                                      -- not tenant-owned; reached only via token hash
645	  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
646	  user_id    INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
647	  token_hash TEXT    NOT NULL UNIQUE,                           -- SHA-256 hex; raw token never stored
648	  created_at TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
649	  expires_at TEXT    NOT NULL,
650	  revoked_at TEXT
651	) STRICT;
652	CREATE INDEX auth_tokens_user ON auth_tokens(user_id) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL;
653	```
654	
655	### Crew, skills and availability
656	
657	```sql
658	CREATE TABLE crew_profiles (
659	  id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,                             -- stable identifier for matcher ties
660	  org_id       INTEGER NOT NULL,
661	  user_id      INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE,                         -- at most one profile per user (P9)
662	  display_name TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (length(trim(display_name)) > 0),   -- P4
663	  bio          TEXT,
664	  created_at   TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
665	  updated_at   TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
666	  UNIQUE (org_id, id),
667	  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, user_id) REFERENCES users(org_id, id)
668	) STRICT;
669	
670	CREATE TABLE skills (
671	  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,                               -- stable across renames (P1)
672	  org_id     INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES organisations(id),
673	  name       TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (length(trim(name)) > 0),
674	  category   TEXT,
675	  created_at TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
676	  UNIQUE (org_id, id),
677	  UNIQUE (org_id, name COLLATE NOCASE)
678	) STRICT;
679	
680	CREATE TABLE crew_skills (
681	  org_id          INTEGER NOT NULL,
682	  crew_profile_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
683	  skill_id        INTEGER NOT NULL,
684	  level           INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (level BETWEEN 1 AND 5),   -- P2
685	  updated_by_id   INTEGER NOT NULL,
686	  updated_at      TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
687	  PRIMARY KEY (crew_profile_id, skill_id),
688	  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, crew_profile_id) REFERENCES crew_profiles(org_id, id),
689	  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, skill_id)        REFERENCES skills(org_id, id),   -- RESTRICT blocks deleting used skills
690	  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, updated_by_id)   REFERENCES users(org_id, id)
691	) STRICT;
692	CREATE INDEX crew_skills_skill ON crew_skills(skill_id);
693	
694	CREATE TABLE unavailability (
695	  id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
696	  org_id          INTEGER NOT NULL,
697	  crew_profile_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
698	  start_date      TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (date(start_date) IS start_date),
699	  end_date        TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (date(end_date) IS end_date),
700	  reason          TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (length(trim(reason)) > 0),   -- P5
701	  created_at      TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (<now>),
702	  CHECK (end_date >= start_date),                                         -- M2
703	  UNIQUE (org_id, id),
704	  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, crew_profile_id) REFERENCES crew_profiles(org_id, id)
705	) STRICT;
706	CREATE INDEX unavailability_profile ON unavailability(crew_profile_id, start_date);
707	```
